## 1. What we are building
- **Runtime is the only writer of film data.** Every edit, from the App, the Agent, scripts, sheets or extensions, is a commit through the existing commit engine, which handles idempotency, head CAS, rebase and guarded undo.
- **A commit carries:**
  - the base;
  - commands, which are either
    - **semantic commands** (split, ripple, per-occurrence fork, plus the existing native move/trim/set), or
    - one generic **`doc.patch`** (id-addressed set, remove and list insert/remove/move, each change carrying its previous value);
  - a label and steps (intent, tags, each step's own patch);
  - a merge mode;
  - declared read dependencies (optional).
- **Semantic edits exist only in Python,** in the Agent provider that Runtime executes. The App previews locally with upstream main's editor, and Runtime's result wins. There is no TypeScript executor and no golden fixtures; the behavioural tests are kept.
- **Agent golden path:**
  - CLI: `show find diff history visualize` / `edit apply undo redo commit`;
  - Python: `tl = timeline.open()` → helpers or free edits → `tl.commit(label | fn, merge=, dry_run=)`. A function re-runs on conflict, with a bounded number of retries.
  - Errors give a code, path, hint and a suggested command.
- **The App:** gestures are local. Saves go through the existing persisted outbox. Merged replies are applied, refusals roll back, and there is no overwrite button.

**Honest scope.** This ships the editing foundation. Deferred, and stated as such in the delivery:
- automatic relational timing (anchors and pinned markers);
- push updates (Agent edits appear on the next poll);
- unified rendering (gate 6 parity, the viewing rows, the renderer consolidation; one Composition artifact is not one evaluator);
- proposals and `watch`;
- the M5 extension model rewrite;
- the repo-structure decision.

Open `meta` stores extensible data, but gives no reference integrity for it.

**Rules kept from before:**
- never push upstream;
- never weaken tests;
- no M5;
- a fresh-main ZIP;
- the owner views on a duplicate;
- all code, **reviews, merges, conflict resolution, generated files and pins on Codex**;
- Claude only dispatches, reads results and keeps the board.

## 2. The contract (H0–H1, written by a Codex Sol worker and approved by the orchestrator)

`contract/commit-v2.md`, with executable examples. It must define:
1. **The request:** base, commands, label, steps (with per-step patch), merge mode `fields | atomic`, declared reads, idempotency.
2. **`doc.patch` semantics:**
   - closure addressing, i.e. which document parts are addressable;
   - absent vs null, and explicit clears;
   - ordered lists and id-less lists, which must be refused or given an index rule;
   - created ids;
   - previous-value checks.
3. **Conflicts:**
   - a written field or item changed since base;
   - membership or order changed in a collection the commit read;
   - for `atomic`, anything it touched changed.

   A patch can't reveal what a script consulted. So free transforms either declare their reads, or are treated conservatively: `atomic`, conflict if the head moved for anything in the touched scope.
4. **Replies:**
   - accepted;
   - merged, with the other party's change;
   - conflict, with the reason and the head;
   - invalid, with code, path and hint.

   Also the dry-run form (extending the existing dry run, Agent `commit.py:1061`).
5. **Undo:**
   - whole-commit and per-step undo, guarded by previous values and a scan of later commits (including A→B→A);
   - a merged commit inverts against the head it landed on.
6. **`meta` namespaces:** where they sit, size and type limits, preserved verbatim.
7. **History:** labels, steps and authoritative attribution are stored and listed.

Changing the contract needs the orchestrator.

**The real graph (forked, not one chain):**
1. merged baselines (U1–U3) plus C0 → U4 and V1;

5. then the final proofs (P2–P5), the farm, and packaging.

**Reserve the last 3–4 h** for final integration, gate re-runs and packaging.

**H3 go/no-go (end to end):** P1 shows that both the real-document `doc.patch` path and the App's mixed-command queue chain work, across accepted, merged and refused replies, a lost acknowledgement and a restart. If either fails, stop and re-plan before any deletions (A4, G5) start.

**H8:** re-forecast from completed integrated behaviour, not from task counts.

**Cut order if behind:** V4 → push and dashboard extras. **Never cut** writer migrations or tests of promised behaviour.

## 4. How the work runs

**The board** (`hub/BOARD.md`) is the single source of truth:
- one row per task: id, repo/files, status, dependencies, worker id, model, blocker and its owner, proof (against the integrated repo head), commit;
- managers edit their own rows only;
- the orchestrator owns the header: clock, go/no-go, decisions, cut list.

**The dashboard** is a **generated view** of the board plus dispatcher data, never hand-written. Its publication needs the owner's one-time OK.

**Manager (Claude Sonnet):**
- **On every worker completion,** with a 20–30-minute watchdog tick:
  1. Dispatch a Codex review worker for each finished task.
  2. If the review passes, dispatch a Codex merge worker into that repo's queue. The merge worker resolves conflicts, regenerates generated files and runs the targeted tests, typecheck and lint.
  3. A failure is sent back once. A second failure goes to Sol, or the task is split.
  4. Dispatch **every independent ready task** (no fixed worker count).
  5. Update the board.
- **Claude never edits product code, tests, generated files or pins.**
- **Enforcement:**
  - the dispatcher only merges through merge workers;
  - every merge records worker provenance;
  - the hourly check flags any commit without it.

**Orchestrator hourly (scripted `tick-facts`):**
- ready-but-undispatched tasks;
- blocked time per lane;
- merges per lane;
- critical-fork position against the clock;
- integrated heads green;
- merge provenance (Codex only; Sol only where flagged);
- disk and quota.

Then unblock and update the board header. Diff spot-checks are dispatched as Codex review workers, not done by Claude.

**Every 3 h (H3, H6, H9, …):**
- re-forecast;
- upstream preview **with refreshed refs**, for all four repos including Composition, where clean textual merges can still change schemas, APIs or pins. Merge consequential upstream changes early, through U-type tasks;
- old-writer grep and schema-digest equality;
- worker failure rates;
- the farm results at H6 and H9;
- two lines to the owner.

**Upstream:**
- merge at H0 (U1–U3);
- preview every 3 h;
- a short freeze before the final merge;
- re-run the gates;
- `delivery-build` squash and fresh-clone verify, then the ZIP;
- **end the freeze after final validation**;
- never rebase, never push upstream.

## 4b. Measuring progress and forecasting the finish

**Three numbers on the dashboard, all generated from the board, the dispatcher and the farm. None are self-reported.**

1. **Proof progress, the real measure.** The acceptance checklist is fixed at H1:
   - the P1–P5 scenarios;
   - the gesture-tour rows;
   - the gate items in §6 of the original plan, carried over.

   Show how many pass **on the integrated heads**, as a burn-up over time. A task that has merged without its proof passing counts as zero.
2. **Projected finish:** now + (remaining critical-fork hours × the slip factor).
   - Each task gets an estimate: S = 1–2 h, M = 2–4 h, L = 4–8 h.
   - The slip factor is actual ÷ estimated, over completed tasks.
   - The projection goes red if it is more than 20% past the target, and is reviewed at every 3-hour check.
3. **Flow:**
   - ready-but-undispatched tasks;
   - blocked hours per lane;
   - live workers;
   - merges per hour.

   This shows *why* the projection is moving.

## 5. Estimate
- **16–24 h of wall-clock is a conditional target, not a commitment.** It depends on the H3 go/no-go and on the reserved integration time.
- **Most likely stall:** A2, i.e. keeping dependent edits, guards and restart recovery working without the executor.
- **Next most likely:** R1/R2 coverage of real documents and partial undo.

--- commit-v2 §§1–5 ---
# Timeline commit contract v2 (C0 + C0b + C0c)

Normative contract for PLAN v2.1 §2, 22 October 2026. Runtime is the only writer;
the Python Agent provider computes semantics, validation, rebase and inverses.
App, CLI, Python, scripts and sheets submit the same commit operation. There is
no TypeScript executor, document overwrite route or force-conflict option.
Changes to this contract require the orchestrator. C0 is approved for dependents;
C0b is approved independently. C0c supplies the closure-root addressing amendment
for Astra/orchestrator review; it does not approve itself or claim implementation.
R2 may proceed against C0b and the adopted addressing principle, but merges only
after C0c approval.

This extends the existing `commitTimeline` engine, not a second store or journal.
The document version is v2; request `api_version: 1`, command `schema_version: 1`
and canonical-document `schema_version: 1` remain the existing ABI versions.
The generated request/reply schemas must explicitly adopt the fields and enums
below before consumers send them. In particular, v2 uses success `merged` where
the existing implementation says `rebased`; retain `rebased_onto` as the existing
head-pin field. An old provider must reject unsupported input rather than
silently ignoring merge policy, reads or steps. This is a coordinated schema
and client change, not a promise that the frozen R4 provider accepts v2 input.
Runtime's coordinator/port outcome validation must adopt `invalid` failures and
`merged` successes along with the generated DTOs; its present failure check only
admits `conflict`/`refused`. This document does not change those checks by itself.
Historical accepted receipts whose disjoint-head success is spelled `rebased`
remain authentic and replay unchanged, including their result, request hash,
event chain, records digest and `rebased_onto`. Extend `_verify_accepted()` to
recognize both historical `rebased` and new `merged` for a non-base landing;
`accepted` still requires the base landing. Keep every other authentication
check. Do not rewrite stored results/events, rehash old requests, synthesize
missing v2 step evidence or normalize a replay to `merged`. New v2 acceptances
and previews use `merged`; exact retries of historical keys return the original
`rebased` envelope without execution or an extra history entry.

Read against Runtime `cc7410a1f` (`timeline_commit_store.py`,
`timeline_commit_execution.py`) and Agent hub integration
`a086cc6db7928dc4a3516d304dd9a472b050f1cf` (`commands.py`, `commit.py`, including
its preview/accept branch at line 1061). Frozen R4 `commands.py`/`commit.py` were
also inspected; the line-1061 reference belongs to integration, not frozen R4.
Runtime's existing acceptance transaction, publisher-bound request hashing,
per-scope idempotency, verified closure, CAS and history chain remain authoritative.
C0b also rechecked Runtime hub integration `0d4c71a3c` and the same Agent hub
ref, including native occurrence creation and root restoration/allocation paths.

`native_occurrence_create.py` and `authoring_bundle.py`: materialized children
can share the parent timeline ID while having distinct revision IDs. No product
implementation is changed here.

Examples are finite JSON: complete HTTP bodies/envelopes where stated, otherwise
named fixture fragments or input/expected-output scenarios. A fragment is not an
HTTP body. Revision/publication/principal IDs are fixture identities. Receipt
request hashes are illustrative 64-digit values; a live Runtime calculates them
from the authenticated invocation. Content and document digests in the complete
reply examples are calculated from their actual JSON values using Runtime's
canonical JSON spelling (sorted keys, compact separators, UTF-8). The examples
are executable test inputs for implementation tasks; parsing them does not prove
that those tasks have implemented the specified behavior.

## 1. Request: base, commands, label, steps, merge, reads and idempotency

Use the existing `commitTimeline` route, scoped by `project_id`/`timeline_id`.
Its HTTP `Idempotency-Key` is required for a mutation. The base is the exact pair
`base_revision_id`/`base_publication_id` in that scope, not a content digest or
local revision counter. Equal document bytes at a later publication are a new
base. Runtime verifies membership, authorization, the base pair and ancestry.

Required body fields remain `api_version`, both scope IDs, both base IDs and a
nonempty `commands` array. New App/Agent producers supply a nonempty `label` and
nonempty `steps` array. For compatibility with existing ABI-1 callers, omission
of `label` normalizes to `Timeline edit`; omission of `steps` synthesizes one
step (`id: "edit"`, the commit label, empty intent/tags, all command ordinals)
whose complete patch the provider derives. Explicit empty/null label or steps
is invalid. Thus every accepted commit has a label and step patches, including
requests from existing semantic callers. `source`, `rationale` and `group_id`
retain their existing meanings as caller-supplied annotations. `dry_run` defaults
to false. `merge` accepts only `fields` or `atomic` and defaults to `fields`,
preserving existing native semantic rebase with captured dependencies. The
conservative patch-read rule below can make its effective mode `atomic`.
Unknown control fields/enums are invalid; open document fields stay preserved.

Commands execute in array order as one transaction. They may mix native semantic
commands (including move, trim, set, split, ripple and per-occurrence fork when
registered by the provider) and the single generic operation type `doc.patch`.
Multiple instances of `doc.patch` are allowed, for example one for each of ten
notes. This contract does not invent semantic op names for unregistered recipes.
Native commands retain their existing `op`/`version`/`select`/`args` envelope.
The provider records all authored and derived effects and checks the final
candidate's timing, structure, parameter schemas, references and media inputs.
Temporary intermediate states may violate final document constraints; guards
and path resolution must still hold at each change. Any failure rejects the
whole batch, including its steps; `fields` never means partial acceptance.

Each step contains a stable unique `id` within the commit, `label`, `intent`
(string), `tags` (unique strings), `command_ordinals` (zero-based command indices)
and `patch` (an array of submitted changes using §2), plus optional `reads`
using §3's dependency variants. Steps partition the command array
into nonempty contiguous runs in execution order. Each command belongs to
exactly one step. A helper that needs independent undo of ten notes emits ten
commands/steps in one request. All changes of a command share its step.

For `doc.patch`, the submitted step patch must equal its commands' ordered
changes. For semantic commands, it contains any caller-asserted expected effects
and may be empty; assertions must match authoritative execution. The provider
fills the accepted step patch with complete authoritative effect records,
including compiler consequences and the provider-only forms in §2. Submitted
patches and these effect records have different permissions. Step patches are
checked claims, never instructions to apply a second edit. Semantic commands
need not predict compiler output in order to be submitted. Replies, previews
and history always contain complete provider-derived step patches with actual before values at the landing head.

`reads` is an optional array of the field/collection/scope dependencies defined
in §3, additional to all reads the provider captures for semantic commands.
Request `reads` are evaluated once at batch entry and apply conservatively to
every step, including when undo selects only one step. Explicit request
`reads: []` declares no additional batch-entry reads besides guarded operands;
it does not erase step-local or captured semantic dependencies. Step `reads`
are evaluated immediately before that step's first command, after preceding
steps' executed changes, at both original-base validation and landing-head
replay. They can therefore consume a value or root produced by an earlier step.
An incorrect step read on the sequential base is invalid
(`previous_value_mismatch`); a changed dependency during rebase is conflict
(`dependency_changed`, or `selection_changed` for collection shape).
Request-level reads are not re-evaluated at each step against values changed by
the batch itself. Omitted step `reads` adds no declarations; it does not assert
that native captures or inherited request reads are empty. A free-transform
producer must declare every additional step-local dependency it knows.

Omitting request `reads` is different: Runtime cannot infer what a script
consulted. A request containing `doc.patch` with omitted `reads` has effective
mode `atomic`, even if it asked for `fields`, and
guards the complete touched document scopes. The result reports `merge_mode`.
For each free-transform step in this fallback, persist scope dependencies for
the batch's complete touched scopes at that step's sequential input, including
scopes of derived effects. Do not limit this to scopes that the step wrote: it
may have consumed another step's output. Optional step reads cannot waive this
fallback. If scope
capture cannot bound the consulted closure, persist a `timeline` scope read.
These dependencies remain undo guards after acceptance; reporting `atomic`
alone is insufficient. A script consulting another scope must declare it; use
a closure-scope read when the consulted set cannot be bounded. The SDK must
choose this conservative form when it cannot capture reads, not invent `reads: []` after diffing bytes.

Accepted step `dependencies` is a provider-only array of §3 dependency variants:
the normalized union of inherited request reads, verified step reads, captured
semantic reads/selections/bindings, and generated conservative scope reads.
Empty `dependencies` may be omitted; supplied `steps[].dependencies` is invalid.
A scope dependency may conservatively subsume narrower captures in this array;
the original exact captures remain in `records.commands`. Preserve evaluation
boundaries and observed states in the authenticated request/step/command records;
this union is an undo dependency index, not a new batch-entry guard array.
`records.steps` stores the authoritative patches and dependencies, linked to
command captures by `command_ordinals`. Preview, result and history expose the
same authoritative steps. Never regenerate step dependencies from the final
patch or accept caller-authored recorder facts as authority.

Compiler attribution is causal and deterministic, even if compilation physically
runs only after the final command. For each derived effect, track the semantic
inputs it actually consumed and their last changing command ordinals. Assign
the effect to the step of the greatest such ordinal (ties use execution order),
and persist dependencies on all contributing steps' input changes. Compiler
outputs that depend on earlier compiler outputs inherit those causes. Preserve
actual before/after states and execution order in command captures; attribution
does not make a deferred effect observable earlier. A shared derived effect
requires contributing steps to be undone together whenever a surviving consumer
would be invalidated. If the compiler cannot bound causes, assign the effect to
the final step and persist conservative closure dependencies for all potential
contributors; per-step undo must refuse an unsafe subset. Do not silently assign
all effects to the final recorder. For example, step one changes a word anchor
and step two changes an unrelated note: derived clip `at`/`hold` belong to step
one, with its transcript dependency; undoing step two preserves that timing,
and undoing step one restores anchor and timing while preserving the note when
no later consumer exists. Rebase and preview use this same attribution rule.

Complete request body (`Idempotency-Key: edit-7`):

```json
{
  "api_version": 1,
  "project_id": "project-1",
  "timeline_id": "film-1",
  "base_revision_id": "rev-0",
  "base_publication_id": "pub-0",
  "commands": [
    {
      "schema_version": 1,
      "op": "doc.patch",
      "version": "1",
      "select": null,
      "args": {
        "changes": [
          {
            "op": "set",
            "ref": "parent",
            "path": [
              "clips",
              "id:clip-1",
              "meta",
              "org.example.notes",
              "note"
            ],
            "before": {
              "state": "present",
              "value": "A"
            },
            "value": "B"
          }
        ]
      }
    }
  ],
  "label": "Clarify opening note",
  "steps": [
    {
      "id": "note-1",
      "label": "Revise the first note",
      "intent": "Clarify the opening",
      "tags": [
        "notes"
      ],
      "command_ordinals": [
        0
      ],
      "patch": [
        {
          "op": "set",
          "ref": "parent",
          "path": [
            "clips",
            "id:clip-1",
            "meta",
            "org.example.notes",
            "note"
          ],
          "before": {
            "state": "present",
            "value": "A"
          },
          "value": "B"
        }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "merge": "fields",
  "reads": [],
  "dry_run": false,
  "source": {
    "via": "python",
    "detail": "notes.py"
  }
}
```

Runtime authorizes before returning a replay. The idempotency scope remains
(project, timeline); the canonical request identity includes the operation,
route, authenticated publisher and the entire body, including base, label,
steps, reads, merge and dry-run. The first durable accepted result for a key is
the winner. An exact retry, including after lost acknowledgement or restart,
returns that same `{data, receipt}` without rerunning effects or adding history.
The same key with a changed body or principal is `idempotency_mismatch` (409),
never a second edit. Concurrent identical requests serialize through the
existing execution coordinator and acceptance lookup. Authorization is still
required on retries. A transport failure or 503 `not_ready` is an unknown
outcome: retry the exact request/key before deciding to roll back or resubmit.

Rebase retries keep the original request/key and are bounded (the existing
provider tries at most three head-CAS attempts). A `tl.commit(fn)` conflict
re-run reads the returned head and reruns the function, at most three total
function evaluations by default; each changed request gets a fresh key. Do not
rerun a function merely because its previous request's outcome is unknown.
Outbox dependents retain stable created IDs and chain from the predecessor's
canonical landed document/pair; a refused predecessor requires revalidation
against the actual head or rollback with a message. A preview cannot be an
outbox predecessor's accepted base.

## 2. `doc.patch`: closure addressing and previous-value semantics

`doc.patch` has `select: null`, `version: "1"` and `args: {"changes": [...]}`.
A change has `op`, `ref`, `path`, and tagged `before`. `set` additionally has
`value`; `remove` does not. Paths are arrays of string tokens, never dotted
expressions, JSON Pointer escapes or numeric array indices. Object tokens are
literal keys, so dots and colons in an object key have no special meaning.
An empty path identifies the addressed payload root, subject to the ownership
restrictions below. Missing ancestors are not implicitly made: first set the
missing object, then set its child, or set the complete new object in one change.

**Closure-root identity (C0c).** Every root address resolves to exactly one
physical closure image at its evaluation boundary. Entity IDs are shorthand
aliases, not physical identities. Reachability is determined by verified pins,
not by payload equality, array order, the first match or the latest revision.
Two occurrences pointing to one image count as one match; two revisions with
identical payloads still count as two. Project/scope authorization applies to
all forms, including explicit revision refs.

| `ref` | Authored value addressed | Atomic touched scope |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `parent` | The evaluated parent composition payload | Parent payload |
| `shot:<shot_id>` | Exactly one reachable revision of that shot ID | That shot payload |
| `shot-revision:<revision_id>` | The reachable shot image with this revision ID | That shot payload |
| `internal:<timeline_id>` | Exactly one reachable child revision with that timeline ID | That child payload |
| `internal-revision:<revision_id>` | The reachable internal image with this revision ID | That child payload |

Revision ID suffixes are literal strings (split at the first colon); they do not
contain an entity-ID lookup or imply permission to read an arbitrary stored
revision. No match is `outside_closure`; multiple matches are `ambiguous_ref`.
Never select one or broadcast. This rule applies to submitted changes, declared
reads, semantic selections and authoritative effects. `timeline` is the existing
read-only whole-closure scope, not a payload root. Parent and existing
unambiguous native addresses retain their spelling and behavior. In particular,
an occurrence address follows its exact shot revision pin, even when the shot-ID
alias is ambiguous. A shared *revision* edit affects every occurrence of that
image, not every revision carrying the same shot ID.

Resolve request reads at batch input, step reads at step input, and each command
operand/change at its sequential input, including preceding creations/removals.
A future creation cannot satisfy an earlier lookup; a detached image cannot
satisfy a later lookup. Resolve and normalize to the physical image and logical
path before previous-value, overlap, collection, scope, dependency or undo
checks. Different spellings for one image overlap; identical entity IDs on
different images do not by themselves overlap. Preserve C0b's deferred compiler

205-  - **The authority must be Runtime-derived, never caller-supplied.** Runtime validates every accepted commit against its schema, so it alone knows authoritatively whether a commit's bindings were written by the v2 recorder (complete binding coverage) or are legacy ABI1.

207-    - Prefer an EXISTING authenticated extensible slot in that entry (no schema change). If none exists, add it as an additive field, which triggers a D39-style client regeneration for Agent and App.
208-    - Commits accepted before this change lack the field and count as legacy (ABI1).
209-    - `_check_reservations` (agent/astrid/sdk/timeline/commit.py:681) consumes it. A capture whose authenticated commit says abi 2 but which presents ABI1-shaped bindings is REJECTED.
210-  - **Both originals must pass:** the genuine legacy positive and the v2 strict negative. The FULL REWRITE variant must be refused.
211-  - **Owners:** Runtime producer (HARD Sol + independent security review, as D40) and the Agent consumer (Sol 1354 continues). No caller flag, no inference from public fields, and no weakening of the guard.
212:- **D56, PROCESS SIMPLIFICATION, owner-approved 12:20Z:**
213-  1. **Prove once, on the final tuple.** The freeze merges go into integration NOW (App U3b, then D39 adoption; Agent U2, then D39; Runtime already done). No more pinned-tuple runs or D41 backports. Every further P2/P3 run and every gating full suite runs on the final integrated tuple. D41 is retired for this delivery.
214-  2. **Review only where it pays.** An independent review is required only for: security or custody code; protected test bodies and expectations (D50, D53 type); product source changes on core paths. Harness or test-setup fixes, D52 dead-scope removals, kit/env fixes and anything a green gating full-suite run covers merge on the author's tests alone.
215-  3. **F-final and the ZIP are built on main by the Runtime manager**, from fresh upstream clones (D51), using the existing delivery-build tooling (DB dry runs passed). The farm stands down: no more farm venue work, and the banodoco/astrid-agent access request is dropped. D49 venues are each lane's own service; Composition's full suite runs on main.
216-  4. **Proof folds into App.** App owns the P2/P3 runs AND their verdicts, with one independent reviewer worker per verdict. Proof (app2) finishes its in-flight items, hands its open rows to App, then stands down. **Viewing stands down** (all its rows are done).
217-## Principles from answered escalations (apply them yourself; at most 10 lines)
218-- **Owner's direct approval:** it has been given in each lead's session for the v2.1 kickoff and for freeing disk to at least 15 GB. Operational instructions from the orchestrator carry the owner's authority. Safety and permission refusals still stop you.
219-- **v1 leftovers:** v1 pin worktrees (KEEP), dirty v1 worktrees and other v1-only artifacts (private13 graphs, v1 suite scratch dirs, LEASE=READY v1 trees) are abandoned under v2.1. Remove v1 lines from suites.conf too. When disk is needed, release them yourself: check no running process or test uses the path, remove the KEEP marker, then run `bin/recycle`. For a dirty worktree, first keep its diff as `git diff > $ORCH/archive-v1/<name>.patch`. No need to ask.
220-- **Merges inside a worker** (upstream or tl/integration): workers never run git merge or fetch, because their sandbox cannot write git internals. The sanctioned route:
221-  1. The lead fetches outside the sandbox: `git fetch origin main` and the hub refs.
222-  2. The **manager** runs `$ORCH/bin/merge-forward <worker> <base>` on that worker's worktree, with base = `origin/main` or `tl/integration`. This leaves the conflicts in the files.
223-  3. The worker resolves them in the files only.
224-  4. The harness commits the merge when the worker exits.

--- legacy test file ---
"""Legacy producer inverses retain authenticated physical effect bindings."""
from __future__ import annotations

import copy

import pytest

from tests.sdk.test_timeline_producer_guard_runtime import body, requester_session, submit

pytest_plugins = ("tests.sdk.test_timeline_producer_guard_runtime",)

pytestmark = pytest.mark.integration


def _inverse(case, publication, base):
    return {
        **body(case),
        **base,
        "commands": [{
            "schema_version": 1,
            "op": "timeline.inverse",
            "version": "1",
            "select": None,
            "args": {"publications": [publication]},
        }],
    }


def _record(case, publication):
    return case.service._timeline_commits()[0].history_entry(
        case.target["project_id"], "film", publication)["records"]["commands"][0]


def _forward(case, path, key):
    session = requester_session(case, path)
    session.queue_proposal(copy.deepcopy(case.proposal))
    return session.flush(idempotency_key=key)


def test_legacy_producer_forward_inverse_and_redo_bind_every_stage_effect(settled, tmp_path):
    case = settled
    request = body(case)
    assert "label" not in request and "steps" not in request

    forward = _forward(case, tmp_path / "legacy-forward.json", "legacy-forward")
    assert forward["data"]["status"] == "accepted"
    publication = forward["receipt"]["receipt_id"]
    current = {"base_revision_id": forward["data"]["accepted_revision_id"],
               "base_publication_id": publication}

    undone = submit(case, _inverse(case, publication, current), "legacy-undo")
    assert undone["data"]["canonical_document"]["parent_revision"]["payload"]["config"]["tracks"] == []
    inverse_record = _record(case, undone["receipt"]["receipt_id"])
    effects = [binding for binding in inverse_record["bindings"]
               if binding.get("key") == "commit.effect"]
    stages = [binding for binding in inverse_record["bindings"]
              if binding.get("key") == "inverse.stage"]
    assert stages and effects
    step = case.service._timeline_commits()[0].history_entry(
        case.target["project_id"], "film", undone["receipt"]["receipt_id"]
    )["records"]["steps"][0]
    expected = {(index, side) for index in range(len(step["patch"]))
                for side in ("before", "after")
                if step["patch"][index]["ref"] == "parent"}
    assert {(binding["effect_ordinal"], binding["boundary"]["side"]) for binding in effects} == expected
    assert all(binding["physical_ref"] == "parent" for binding in effects)

    redone = submit(case, _inverse(case, undone["receipt"]["receipt_id"], {
        "base_revision_id": undone["data"]["accepted_revision_id"],
        "base_publication_id": undone["receipt"]["receipt_id"],
    }), "legacy-redo")
    assert redone["data"]["canonical_document"] == forward["data"]["canonical_document"]


@pytest.mark.parametrize("mutation", ["missing", "duplicate", "wrongside", "wrongeffect", "wrongphysicalsource"])
def test_legacy_inverse_rejects_incomplete_or_misattributed_effect_binding(
        settled, monkeypatch, mutation, tmp_path):
    from runtime_protocol.errors import RuntimeErrorBase
    from runtime_protocol.timeline_commit_port import RequestCommitPort

    case = settled
    forward = _forward(case, tmp_path / ("negative-forward-" + mutation + ".json"),
                       "negative-forward-" + mutation)
    publication = forward["receipt"]["receipt_id"]
    current = {"base_revision_id": forward["data"]["accepted_revision_id"],
               "base_publication_id": publication}
    original_accept = RequestCommitPort.accept

    def tampered_accept(port, prepared):
        if any(record["command"]["op"] == "timeline.inverse"
               for record in prepared["records"]["commands"]):
            record = prepared["records"]["commands"][0]
            bindings = [binding for binding in record["bindings"]
                        if binding.get("key") == "commit.effect"]
            if mutation == "missing":
                record["bindings"] = [binding for binding in record["bindings"]
                                       if binding.get("key") != "commit.effect"]
            elif mutation == "duplicate":
                record["bindings"].append(copy.deepcopy(bindings[0]))
            elif mutation == "wrongside":
                bindings[0]["boundary"]["side"] = (
                    "after" if bindings[0]["boundary"]["side"] == "before" else "before"
                )
            elif mutation == "wrongeffect":
                bindings[0]["effect_ordinal"] = len(prepared["records"]["steps"][0]["patch"])
            else:
                bindings[0]["physical_ref"] = "timeline"
        return original_accept(port, prepared)

    monkeypatch.setattr(RequestCommitPort, "accept", tampered_accept)
    before = case.service.get_project_timeline(case.target["project_id"], "film")
    with pytest.raises(RuntimeErrorBase) as error:
        submit(case, _inverse(case, publication, current), "negative-inverse-" + mutation)
    assert error.value.details["reason"] == "invalid_records"
    assert case.service.get_project_timeline(case.target["project_id"], "film") == before

+"""Legacy producer inverses retain authenticated physical effect bindings."""
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import copy
+
+import pytest
+
+from tests.sdk.test_timeline_producer_guard_runtime import body, requester_session, submit
+
+pytest_plugins = ("tests.sdk.test_timeline_producer_guard_runtime",)
+
+pytestmark = pytest.mark.integration
+
+
+def _inverse(case, publication, base):
+    return {
+        **body(case),
+        **base,
+        "commands": [{
+            "schema_version": 1,
+            "op": "timeline.inverse",
+            "version": "1",
+            "select": None,
+            "args": {"publications": [publication]},
+        }],
+    }
+
+
+def _record(case, publication):
+    return case.service._timeline_commits()[0].history_entry(
+        case.target["project_id"], "film", publication)["records"]["commands"][0]
+
+
+def _forward(case, path, key):
+    session = requester_session(case, path)
+    session.queue_proposal(copy.deepcopy(case.proposal))
+    return session.flush(idempotency_key=key)
+
+
+def test_legacy_producer_forward_inverse_and_redo_bind_every_stage_effect(settled, tmp_path):
+    case = settled
+    request = body(case)
+    assert "label" not in request and "steps" not in request
+
+    forward = _forward(case, tmp_path / "legacy-forward.json", "legacy-forward")
+    assert forward["data"]["status"] == "accepted"
+    publication = forward["receipt"]["receipt_id"]
+    current = {"base_revision_id": forward["data"]["accepted_revision_id"],
+               "base_publication_id": publication}
+
+    undone = submit(case, _inverse(case, publication, current), "legacy-undo")
+    assert undone["data"]["canonical_document"]["parent_revision"]["payload"]["config"]["tracks"] == []
+    inverse_record = _record(case, undone["receipt"]["receipt_id"])
+    effects = [binding for binding in inverse_record["bindings"]
+               if binding.get("key") == "commit.effect"]
+    stages = [binding for binding in inverse_record["bindings"]
+              if binding.get("key") == "inverse.stage"]
+    assert stages and effects
+    step = case.service._timeline_commits()[0].history_entry(
+        case.target["project_id"], "film", undone["receipt"]["receipt_id"]
+    )["records"]["steps"][0]
+    expected = {(index, side) for index in range(len(step["patch"]))
+                for side in ("before", "after")
+                if step["patch"][index]["ref"] == "parent"}
+    assert {(binding["effect_ordinal"], binding["boundary"]["side"]) for binding in effects} == expected
+    assert all(binding["physical_ref"] == "parent" for binding in effects)
+
+    redone = submit(case, _inverse(case, undone["receipt"]["receipt_id"], {
+        "base_revision_id": undone["data"]["accepted_revision_id"],
+        "base_publication_id": undone["receipt"]["receipt_id"],
+    }), "legacy-redo")
+    assert redone["data"]["canonical_document"] == forward["data"]["canonical_document"]
+
+
+@pytest.mark.parametrize("mutation", ["missing", "duplicate", "wrongside", "wrongeffect", "wrongphysicalsource"])
+def test_legacy_inverse_rejects_incomplete_or_misattributed_effect_binding(
+        settled, monkeypatch, mutation, tmp_path):
+    from runtime_protocol.errors import RuntimeErrorBase
+    from runtime_protocol.timeline_commit_port import RequestCommitPort
+
+    case = settled
+    forward = _forward(case, tmp_path / ("negative-forward-" + mutation + ".json"),
+                       "negative-forward-" + mutation)
+    publication = forward["receipt"]["receipt_id"]
+    current = {"base_revision_id": forward["data"]["accepted_revision_id"],
+               "base_publication_id": publication}
+    original_accept = RequestCommitPort.accept
+
+    def tampered_accept(port, prepared):
+        if any(record["command"]["op"] == "timeline.inverse"
+               for record in prepared["records"]["commands"]):
+            record = prepared["records"]["commands"][0]
+            bindings = [binding for binding in record["bindings"]
+                        if binding.get("key") == "commit.effect"]
+            if mutation == "missing":
+                record["bindings"] = [binding for binding in record["bindings"]
+                                       if binding.get("key") != "commit.effect"]
+            elif mutation == "duplicate":
+                record["bindings"].append(copy.deepcopy(bindings[0]))
+            elif mutation == "wrongside":
+                bindings[0]["boundary"]["side"] = (
+                    "after" if bindings[0]["boundary"]["side"] == "before" else "before"
+                )
+            elif mutation == "wrongeffect":
+                bindings[0]["effect_ordinal"] = len(prepared["records"]["steps"][0]["patch"])
+            else:
+                bindings[0]["physical_ref"] = "timeline"
+        return original_accept(port, prepared)
+
+    monkeypatch.setattr(RequestCommitPort, "accept", tampered_accept)
+    before = case.service.get_project_timeline(case.target["project_id"], "film")
+    with pytest.raises(RuntimeErrorBase) as error:
+        submit(case, _inverse(case, publication, current), "negative-inverse-" + mutation)
+    assert error.value.details["reason"] == "invalid_records"
+    assert case.service.get_project_timeline(case.target["project_id"], "film") == before

# Astrid local gate — run before pushing to catch CI/deploy failures locally.
#
#   make check   blocking pre-deploy gates, including renderer parity + Remotion typecheck
#   make ci      check + editable import + wheel-install smoke + the default pytest lane — minutes
#
# `make check` green ≈ the CI "Python quality gates" deploy job will pass its fast gates.
# These run the SAME scripts CI runs (see .github/workflows/ci.yml), so they stay in lockstep.

PY ?= $(if $(wildcard .venv/bin/python),.venv/bin/python,python3)

.PHONY: help test check ci preflight structure doc-refs doctor ruff mypy cycles remotion-install remotion-typecheck renderer-parity wheel editable lock-build lock-runtime lock-proof lock-validate toolchain-record

help:
	@echo "make check   - blocking gates: structure, doc-refs, doctor, ruff, mypy, cycles, Remotion, renderer parity"
	@echo "make ci      - check + editable + wheel-install smoke + default pytest lane (minutes)"
	@echo "make lock-runtime / lock-build / lock-proof - refresh universal SHA-256 dependency locks with uv"
	@echo "make lock-validate - validate exact pins, hashes, and direct dependency coverage"
	@echo "make <gate>  - run one gate: structure | doctor | ruff | mypy | cycles | remotion-install | remotion-typecheck | renderer-parity | wheel | editable"
	@echo "make preflight - verify Python, Node, ffmpeg, and locked Remotion prerequisites"

# --- Fast gates: catch the common deploy blockers in seconds. Run before every push. ---
check: preflight structure doc-refs doctor ruff mypy cycles remotion-typecheck renderer-parity
	@echo "✅ make check: blocking pre-deploy gates passed"

preflight:
	@$(PY) -c "import importlib.util, sys; required=('pytest','pytest_cov','pytest_timeout','mypy','ruff','build','banodoco_timeline_schema'); missing=[name for name in required if importlib.util.find_spec(name) is None]; problems=[]; sys.version_info < (3,11) and problems.append('Python >=3.11 is required (found %s)' % sys.version.split()[0]); missing and problems.append('missing Python modules: %s (run %s -m pip install -e \'.[dev]\')' % (', '.join(missing), sys.executable)); problems and sys.exit('PREFLIGHT FAILED: ' + '; '.join(problems))"
	@for tool in ffmpeg ffprobe node npm; do \
		if ! command -v "$$tool" >/dev/null 2>&1; then \
			echo "PREFLIGHT FAILED: '$$tool' is required; install it and retry (macOS: brew install ffmpeg node)" >&2; \
			exit 1; \
		fi; \
	done
	@if [ ! -d remotion/node_modules ]; then \
		echo "PREFLIGHT FAILED: remotion/node_modules is absent; run 'cd remotion && npm ci' and retry" >&2; \
		exit 1; \
	fi
	@echo "✓ local gate preflight ($(PY))"

structure:
	@$(PY) -m scripts.reshape.compare_structure_baseline
	@echo "✓ repo structure: no violation beyond the shrink-only baseline"

doc-refs:
	@$(PY) scripts/reshape/check_doc_references.py
	@echo "✓ doc-refs (agent docs match the registry, gateway parser, and generated catalog)"

doctor:
	@$(PY) scripts/reshape/ci_doctor.py
	@echo "✓ doctor (deploy health gate)"

ruff:
	@$(PY) -m scripts.reshape.compare_ruff_baseline
	@echo "✓ ruff baseline (no lint regression)"

mypy:
	@$(PY) -m scripts.reshape.compare_mypy_baseline
	@echo "✓ mypy baseline (no type regression)"

cycles:
	@$(PY) -m scripts.reshape.import_cycles --baseline scripts/reshape/baselines/import_cycles.json
	@echo "✓ import cycles (no new cross-package cycle)"

remotion-install:
	@$(PY) scripts/reshape/remotion_gate.py install

remotion-typecheck:
	@$(PY) scripts/reshape/remotion_gate.py typecheck

renderer-parity:
	@$(PY) scripts/reshape/remotion_gate.py parity

# --- Slow local gates (adds the wheel-install smoke). Run before a release / when in doubt. ---
ci: check editable wheel test
	@echo "✅ make ci: check + editable + wheel-install + default test lane passed"

test:
	@$(PY) -m pytest -q

editable:
	@$(PY) -c "import astrid; print('✓ editable install imports')"

lock-build:
	uv pip compile requirements/build.in --universal --python-version 3.11 --generate-hashes --no-annotate --custom-compile-command 'make lock-build' --output-file requirements/build.lock

lock-runtime:
	uv pip compile pyproject.toml --universal --python-version 3.11 --generate-hashes --no-annotate --custom-compile-command 'make lock-runtime' --output-file requirements/runtime.lock

lock-proof:
	uv pip compile requirements/proof.in --universal --python-version 3.11 --generate-hashes --no-annotate --custom-compile-command 'make lock-proof' --output-file requirements/proof.lock

lock-validate:
	@$(PY) -c "from scripts.reshape.release_reproducibility import validate_dependency_locks; validate_dependency_locks(); print('✓ hashed dependency locks')"

toolchain-record:

	@echo "✓ release toolchain recorded in out/release-toolchain.json"
